127	%% ====================================================================
128	\subsection{Cross-Domain Variation}
129	\label{sec:cross_domain}
130	
131	The preceding experiments use C4 (general web text).
132	To test domain dependence, we evaluate on Wikipedia ($n{=}5{,}000$) and arXiv abstracts ($n{=}2{,}200$) across five architecture families (Table~\ref{tab:s12_domain_ablation}).
133	Wikipedia yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ for Qwen-1.5B (1v2 $\auc = 0.619$; Table~\ref{tab:domain_kstar}), but $\Kempirical{=}1$ for LLaMA-8B and Mistral-7B (1v2 $\auc = 0.592,\,0.558$; both below $\theta$).
134	Qwen-1.5B, Pythia-1.4B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, and Mistral-7B yield $\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv (Table~\ref{tab:domain_kstar}): 2v3 $\auc = 0.644\;[0.629,\,0.659]$, $0.648\;[0.634,\,0.662]$, $0.622\;[0.605,\,0.635]$, and $0.628$ respectively (permutation $p < 0.001$), demonstrating that structured academic text permits discrimination to greater depth independently of architecture.
135	OLMo-1B is the sole exception ($\Kempirical{=}1$ across all domains; cross-scorer validated, $p{=}0.510$); Mistral-7B exhibits a monotonic staircase (Wiki${=}$1, C4${=}$1, Code${=}$2, arXiv${=}$3), confirming that $\Kempirical$ is domain-determined.
136	The elevated signal reflects higher per-generation distributional shift (1v2 Jensen--Shannon divergence (JSD) $= 0.077$ vs.\ ${\sim}0.01$ for C4), as formulaic structure amplifies deviation from domain norms.
137	We additionally evaluate on CodeSearchNet Python ($n{=}5{,}000$, continuation mode); all five tested models (Qwen-1.5B, Qwen-7B, LLaMA-8B, Mistral-7B, OLMo-1B) yield $\Kempirical \leq 2$ (Table~\ref{tab:domain_kstar}), matching the C4 baseline.
138	
139	\begin{table}[t]
140	\centering
141	\footnotesize
142	\caption{Cross-domain $\Kempirical$ comparison. $\Kempirical{\leq}2$ for C4 and Code (most families $\Kempirical{=}2$; OLMo-1B: 1); $\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv (four families); Wikipedia varies (1--2). Mistral-7B spans all four domains, exhibiting a monotonic staircase. Bonferroni-corrected 99.5\% CI reported for the boundary pair where available (2v3 for $\Kempirical{=}3$; 1v2 otherwise).}
143	\label{tab:domain_kstar}
144	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
145	\begin{tabular}{llcccc}
146	\toprule
147	\textbf{Domain} & \textbf{Model} & \textbf{1v2 $\auc$} & \textbf{2v3 $\auc$} & \textbf{Bonf CI} & $\Kempirical$ \\
148	\midrule
149	arXiv & Qwen-1.5B\rlap{$^\dagger$}  & .858 & .644 & [.629,\,.659] & 3 \\
150	arXiv & Pythia-1.4B & .805 & .648 & [.634,\,.662] & 3 \\
151	arXiv & LLaMA-8B    & .845 & .622 & [.605,\,.635] & 3 \\
152	arXiv & Mistral-7B  & .820 & .628 & --- & 3 \\
153	arXiv & OLMo-1B\rlap{$^\S$}    & .500 & .497 & [.483,\,.517] & 1 \\
154	C4    & Qwen-1.5B\rlap{$^\dagger$}  & .626 & .550 & --- & 2 \\
155	C4    & Mistral-7B  & .570 & .534 & --- & 1 \\
156	Code  & Qwen-1.5B\rlap{$^\dagger$}  & .738 & .530 & --- & 2 \\
157	Code  & Qwen-7B    & .727 & .537 & [.715,\,.739] & 2 \\
158	Code  & LLaMA-8B   & .739 & .511 & --- & 2 \\
159	Code  & Mistral-7B  & .615 & .529 & [.602,\,.628] & 2 \\
160	Code  & OLMo-1B\rlap{$^\S$}    & .557 & .500 & --- & 1 \\
161	Wiki  & Qwen-1.5B\rlap{$^\dagger$}  & .619 & .550 & --- & 2 \\
162	Wiki  & LLaMA-8B\rlap{$^\ddagger$}  & .592 & .538 & [.579,\,.605] & 1 \\
163	Wiki  & Mistral-7B  & .558 & .519 & [.543,\,.572] & 1 \\
164	\bottomrule
165	\multicolumn{6}{l}{\footnotesize $^\dagger$Qwen2.5-1.5B (base, continuation mode).} \\
166	\multicolumn{6}{l}{\footnotesize $^\ddagger$Marginal: point estimate $< \theta$; CI upper bound crosses 0.60.} \\
167	\multicolumn{6}{l}{\footnotesize $^\S$Sole exception: $K^*{=}1$ across all domains; cross-scorer validated ($p{=}0.510$, n.s.).}
168	\end{tabular}}
169	\end{table}
170	
171	\paragraph{Robustness.}
172	\label{sec:robustness}
173	$\Kempirical{=}2$ holds across segment lengths of 128, 256, and 512 tokens (Table~\ref{tab:s11_length_ablation}); at 512 tokens the 1v2 CI lower bound falls marginally below $\theta$ but 2v3 remains well below $\theta$ at all lengths ($\leq 0.572$).
174	Seed robustness testing (seed $= 123$ vs.\ primary $= 42$; Table~\ref{tab:seed_robustness}) confirms $\Kempirical$ stability in 2/3 configurations; the one marginal shift (Qwen-Wikipedia $\Kempirical{=}2 \to 3$, 2v3 $\auc = 0.607$) has one of five folds below $\theta$.
175	The ${\pm}9$\,pp absolute AUC variation across seeds strengthens the case for threshold-based $\Kempirical$ over raw AUC comparisons.
176

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_015erbtVtn3kHJRs3wLmQSni`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:why_kstar}
4	\label{sec:one_wash}
5	\label{sec:negative_graduated}
6	\label{sec:repr_dependence}
7	\label{sec:boundary_cases}
8	\label{sec:length_confound}
9	
10	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
11	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\auc \geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\theta$.
12	The dominant distributional shift occurs at generation $0{\to}1$; beyond depth~2, no further signal is exploitable.
13	Converging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\% for depths $\geq$2; frontier LLMs show 8--38\% ($p > 0.05$ vs.\ OBD; Table~\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).
14	Unlike model collapse~\cite{shumailov2024collapse} (cumulative quality degradation), one-wash laundering describes erasure of statistical fingerprints; quality may degrade further after features become indistinguishable.
15	
16	\paragraph{Boundary cases.}
17	Four configurations yield $\Kempirical{=}1$ (1v2 AUC $\leq 0.598$); under Bonferroni correction (Table~\ref{tab:bonferroni_ci}), three of six $\Kempirical{=}2$ cases become marginal (CI lower 0.593--0.599).
18	The split does not track scale alone: Pythia reverses from $\Kempirical{=}2$ at 1.4B to 1 at 6.9B, and boundary cases show sample-size sensitivity (Table~\ref{tab:robustness_10k}).
19	The universal bound $\Kempirical \leq 2$ holds across all thresholds in $[0.59, 0.61]$ (Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta}), though two borderline configurations drop to $\Kempirical{=}1$ at $\theta{=}0.61$.
20	
21	\paragraph{Representation dependence.}
22	$\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent: frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 3$, Pythia yields 2, and OLMo yields 1 (Table~\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}).
23	The 15D features trade one depth level for cross-model consistency (6/10 configurations $\Kempirical{=}2$).
24	A DeBERTa-v3-base upper-bound probe yields $\Kempirical^{\text{DeBERTa}} = 2$ on C4 and Code but 4 on arXiv, placing the cost of generator-agnostic features at one depth level for structured text and zero for web text (Table~\ref{tab:deberta_vs_obd}).
25	Feature rankings are scorer-independent ($\rho > 0.90$), but self-scoring inflates boundary AUC by 4.7--11.5\,pp; cross-scoring provides a conservative estimate (Appendix~\ref{sec:computation_cost}).
26	
27	\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\Kempirical$.}
28	C4 and Code yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ across most families; Wikipedia varies (1--2); arXiv yields $\Kempirical{=}3$ across four families (Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}), with OLMo-1B as the sole exception ($\Kempirical{=}1$ across all domains; cross-scorer validated).
29	Formulaic structure creates larger per-generation shifts (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\ ${\sim}0.01$ for C4); stripping markup preserves $\Kempirical{=}3$ ($\Delta\auc = {-}0.021$), and Code yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ despite rigid syntax, confirming academic conventions as the source.
30	
31	\paragraph{Prompt-chain scope and retrain-chain gap.}
32	Retrain-chain experiments (three families, LoRA) yield $\Kempirical{\geq}4$, extending to $\Kempirical{=}7$ at depth~7, because retraining modifies model parameters.
33	Prompt-chain $\Kempirical$ isolates text-intrinsic degradation; our conclusions do not transfer to retrain-chain settings.
34	Decoding strategy does not alter $\Kempirical{=}2$ despite inflating absolute AUC under greedy decoding (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
35	
36	
```

</details>

Wikipedia varies (1--2); arXiv yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across four families (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), with OLMo-1B as the sole exception ($\\Kempirical{=}1$ across all domains; cross-scorer validated).\nFormulaic structure creates larger per-generation shifts (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\\ ${\\sim}0.01$ for C4); stripping markup preserves $\\Kempirical{=}3$ ($\\Delta\\auc = {-}0.021$), and Code yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ despite rigid syntax, confirming academic conventions as the source.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain scope and retrain-chain gap.}\nRetrain-chain experiments (three families, LoRA) yield $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$, extending to $\\Kempirical{=}7$ at depth~7, because retraining modifies model parameters.\nPrompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ isolates text-intrinsic degradation; our conclusions do not transfer to retrain-chain settings.\nDecoding strategy does not alter $\\Kempirical{=}2$ despite inflating absolute AUC under greedy decoding (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n\n",

  "old_string": "Across 10 prompt-chain configurations spanning six architecture families (1B--8B), $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ for all web text sources; arXiv reaches $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across four families.\nOne-wash laundering concentrates the exploitable signal at the 0v1 boundary: binary curation halves MMLU loss, cross-model transfer retains 80--92\\% accuracy, and feature rankings remain scorer invariant ($\\rho > 0.90$).\nFor corpus curation, binary filtering captures the actionable depth signal; graduated strategies offer no benefit beyond $\\Kempirical$ because deeper comparisons reduce to noise, and the domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$ implies that thresholds should be adapted per domain.\nAs recursive generation tools proliferate, monitoring the contamination boundary across successive web crawl epochs may prove equally important.\nSystematic evaluation beyond 8B, validation under retrain-chain dynamics, and closing the $\\Kempirical$--$\\Ktheory$ gap through richer feature spaces remain open.\nExtending depth estimation to multilingual corpora and longitudinal tracking of $\\Kempirical$ across successive Common Crawl snapshots are natural next steps toward comprehensive corpus auditing.",
  "new_string": "Across 10 prompt-chain configurations spanning six architecture families (1B--8B), $\\Kempirical \\leq 2$ for all web text sources; arXiv reaches $\\Kempirical{=}3$ across four families.\nOne-wash laundering concentrates the exploitable signal at the 0v1 boundary: binary curation halves MMLU loss, cross-model transfer retains 80--92\\% accuracy, and feature rankings remain scorer invariant ($\\rho > 0.90$).\nFor corpus curation, binary filtering captures the actionable depth signal; graduated strategies offer no benefit beyond $\\Kempirical$, and the domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$ implies that thresholds should be adapted per domain.\nSystematic evaluation beyond 8B, validation under retrain-chain dynamics, extending to multilingual corpora, and closing the $\\Kempirical$--$\\Ktheory$ gap remain open."
}

          "-Extending depth estimation to multilingual corpora and longitudinal tracking of $\\Kempirical$ across successive Common Crawl snapshots are natural next steps toward comprehensive corpus auditing.",
          "+For corpus curation, binary filtering captures the actionable depth signal; graduated strategies offer no benefit beyond $\\Kempirical$, and the domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$ implies that thresholds should be adapted per domain.",
          "+Systematic evaluation beyond 8B, validation under retrain-chain dynamics, extending to multilingual corpora, and closing the $\\Kempirical$--$\\Ktheory$ gap remain open."

```text
1	\section{Consistency Check: $\Kstar$-Guided Filtering}
2	\label{sec:filtering}
3	
4	$\Kempirical = 2$ makes a falsifiable prediction: binary curation should suffice, and graduated performance should converge toward binary as the decay steepness increases.
5	We present this as a consistency check under extreme contamination, not as an independent contribution; at realistic rates ($\leq$50\%), strategy differences remain below 0.3\,pp (Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response}).
6	We compare six strategies on 30{,}000 documents (depths 0--5): (i)~\textbf{no-filter}, (ii)~\textbf{binary-ours} ($w = 1 - P(\text{synthetic})$ from 15D LightGBM), (iii)~\textbf{binary-Drayson}~\citep{drayson2025}, (iv)~\textbf{graduated} ($w = \exp(-\alpha \cdot \hat{d})$, $\alpha \in \{0.1,\ldots,0.9\}$), (v)~\textbf{surprisal} (retain above median), and (vi)~\textbf{DSIR}~\citep{xie2023dsir}.
7	We fine-tune Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct via Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA; rank~8, $\alpha_{\text{LoRA}}{=}16$) for 3 epochs on MMLU and HellaSwag across 5 seeds (${\sim}$83\% synthetic; depth~0 retains human seeds~\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}).
8	
9	\begin{table}[t]
10	\centering
11	\small
12	\caption{Downstream task performance (MMLU, HellaSwag) under six data-filtering strategies. Binary-ours uses the 15D LightGBM depth classifier; Graduated applies exponentially decaying weights $w = \exp(-\alpha \hat{d})$. Mean $\pm$ std over 5 seeds. Full $\alpha$ sweep in Figure~\ref{fig:filtering_strategies}.}
13	\label{tab:tab_filtering_main}
14	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\input{tables/tab_filtering_main}}
15	\end{table}
16	
17	\begin{figure}[t]
18	\centering
19	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_filtering_strategies.pdf}
20	\caption{MMLU accuracy under six filtering strategies (5 seeds, $\pm$1 std). Binary $\Kstar$ filtering (red) minimizes degradation; graduated weighting converges toward binary as $\alpha$ increases. Dashed line: no-filter baseline.}
21	\label{fig:filtering_strategies}
22	\end{figure}
23	
24	Binary-ours reduces the MMLU gap from $-$1.53\,pp (no-filter) to $-$0.77\,pp (${\sim}$50\% reduction; $p_{\text{corr}} < 0.05$ vs.\ all alternatives; Table~\ref{tab:tab_filtering_main}, \ref{tab:pairwise_significance}).
25	The ordering is interpretable through $\Kempirical$: binary filtering exploits the one reliable boundary (0v1), while graduated strategies assign weights based on near-random classifier outputs, injecting noise.
26	Graduated filtering converges toward binary: at $\alpha{=}0.9$, it matches binary-ours on HellaSwag ($p_{\text{corr}} = 1.00$) but remains worse on MMLU ($p_{\text{corr}} = 0.011$); DSIR is indistinguishable from no-filter (Figure~\ref{fig:filtering_strategies}).
27	At realistic contamination ratios ($\leq$50\%), strategy differences remain below 0.3\,pp, consistent with one-wash laundering: the human-authored majority dominates the training signal and marginal gains are smaller than inter-seed variance ($\sigma \approx 0.002$; Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response}).
28	This extreme setting validates $\Kempirical$'s predictive power: despite vanishing effect sizes at realistic ratios, the predicted strategy ordering holds.
29	
```

      "content": "\\section{Consistency Check: $\\Kstar$-Guided Filtering}\n\\label{sec:filtering}\n\n$\\Kempirical = 2$ makes a falsifiable prediction: binary curation should suffice, and graduated performance should converge toward binary as the decay steepness increases.\nWe present this as a consistency check under extreme contamination, not as an independent contribution; at realistic rates ($\\leq$50\\%), strategy differences remain below 0.3\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).\nWe compare six strategies on 30{,}000 documents (depths 0--5): (i)~\\textbf{no-filter}, (ii)~\\textbf{binary-ours} ($w = 1 - P(\\text{synthetic})$ from 15D LightGBM), (iii)~\\textbf{binary-Drayson}~\\citep{drayson2025}, (iv)~\\textbf{graduated} ($w = \\exp(-\\alpha \\cdot \\hat{d})$, $\\alpha \\in \\{0.1,\\ldots,0.9\\}$), (v)~\\textbf{surprisal} (retain above median), and (vi)~\\textbf{DSIR}~\\citep{xie2023dsir}.\nWe fine-tune Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct via Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA; rank~8, $\\alpha_{\\text{LoRA}}{=}16$) for 3 epochs on MMLU and HellaSwag across 5 seeds (${\\sim}$83\\% synthetic; depth~0 retains human seeds~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Downstream task performance (MMLU, HellaSwag) under six data-filtering strategies. Binary-ours uses the 15D LightGBM depth classifier; Graduated applies exponentially decaying weights $w = \\exp(-\\alpha \\hat{d})$. Mean $\\pm$ std over 5 seeds. Full $\\alpha$ sweep in Figure~\\ref{fig:filtering_strategies}.}\n\\label{tab:tab_filtering_main}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\input{tables/tab_filtering_main}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_filtering_strategies.pdf}\n\\caption{MMLU accuracy under six filtering strategies (5 seeds, $\\pm$1 std). Binary $\\Kstar$ filtering (red) minimizes degradation; graduated weighting converges toward binary as $\\alpha$ increases. Dashed line: no-filter baseline.}\n\\label{fig:filtering_strategies}\n\\end{figure}\n\nBinary-ours reduces the MMLU gap from $-$1.53\\,pp (no-filter) to $-$0.77\\,pp (${\\sim}$50\\% reduction; $p_{\\text{corr}} < 0.05$ vs.\\ all alternatives; Table~\\ref{tab:tab_filtering_main}, \\ref{tab:pairwise_significance}).\nThe ordering is interpretable through $\\Kempirical$: binary filtering exploits the one reliable boundary (0v1), while graduated strategies assign weights based on near-random classifier outputs, injecting noise.\nGraduated filtering converges toward binary: at $\\alpha{=}0.9$, it matches binary-ours on HellaSwag ($p_{\\text{corr}} = 1.00$) but remains worse on MMLU ($p_{\\text{corr}} = 0.011$); DSIR is indistinguishable from no-filter (Figure~\\ref{fig:filtering_strategies}).\nAt realistic contamination ratios ($\\leq$50\\%), strategy differences remain below 0.3\\,pp, consistent with one-wash laundering: the human-authored majority dominates the training signal and marginal gains are smaller than inter-seed variance ($\\sigma \\approx 0.002$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).\nThis extreme setting validates $\\Kempirical$'s predictive power: despite vanishing effect sizes at realistic ratios, the predicted strategy ordering holds.\n",
      "numLines": 29,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 29
    }
  },

  "content": "[Director 指令] [Patch] 两个问题需要修复：\n\n**问题 1：θ=0.61 的 \"two borderline cases\" 表述不准确**\n正文说 \"at θ=0.61, two borderline cases shift to K*=1\"。但从实际数据看：\n- Pythia 1v2=0.6074 → 在 θ=0.61 时确实掉（0.6074 > 0.61 不成立... 等等，0.6074 < 0.61 不对，0.6074 > 0.61 不成立因为 0.6074 < 0.61 也不对。让我重新算：0.6074 < 0.61? 不，0.6074 > 0.61 不成立，因为 0.6074 是大于 0.60 但小于 0.61 的... 不对，0.6074 > 0.61? 0.6074 > 0.61 → False，所以 Pythia 在 θ=0.61 确实掉)\n- Qwen-cont 1v2=0.6112 → 0.6112 > 0.61 → True，不会掉\n- LLaMA 1v2=0.6123 → 0.6123 > 0.61 → True，不会掉\n\n所以在 θ=0.61 时可能只有 Pythia（以及其他 CI lower < 0.61 的配置）会掉，不一定是\"两个\"。请用 registry 中各配置的精确 1v2 AUC 值（不要四舍五入）逐一检查哪些配置在 θ=0.61 时 AUC ≤ θ，然后修正 \"two borderline cases\" 为准确数字。如果不确定精确数值，改用更安全的表述如 \"borderline configurations may shift to K*=1\"。\n\n**问题 2：Table 14 \"three families\" vs 正文 \"四组\" 不一致**\n正文 Section 3.2 说 15 个 features 分成四组（surprisal moments / surprisal derivatives / vocabulary diversity / frequency tail statistics）。但 Table 14 caption 说 \"three families\"。\n修复方向：统一为一致的分类。建议改 Table 14 caption 为 \"four groups\"（与正文一致），或者如果 Table 14 确实只列了三大类（surprisal 含 moments+derivatives 作为一类），则正文也要相应调整。确保全文 feature taxonomy 一致。\n\n编译确认后汇报。这次不用验证引用。"

_source line 1399 · 2026-05-25T06:18:48.860Z_

[Director 指令] [Patch] 两个问题需要修复：

**问题 1：θ=0.61 的 "two borderline cases" 表述不准确**
正文说 "at θ=0.61, two borderline cases shift to K*=1"。但从实际数据看：
- Pythia 1v2=0.6074 → 在 θ=0.61 时确实掉（0.6074 > 0.61 不成立... 等等，0.6074 < 0.61 不对，0.6074 > 0.61 不成立因为 0.6074 < 0.61 也不对。让我重新算：0.6074 < 0.61? 不，0.6074 > 0.61 不成立，因为 0.6074 是大于 0.60 但小于 0.61 的... 不对，0.6074 > 0.61? 0.6074 > 0.61 → False，所以 Pythia 在 θ=0.61 确实掉)
- Qwen-cont 1v2=0.6112 → 0.6112 > 0.61 → True，不会掉
- LLaMA 1v2=0.6123 → 0.6123 > 0.61 → True，不会掉

所以在 θ=0.61 时可能只有 Pythia（以及其他 CI lower < 0.61 的配置）会掉，不一定是"两个"。请用 registry 中各配置的精确 1v2 AUC 值（不要四舍五入）逐一检查哪些配置在 θ=0.61 时 AUC ≤ θ，然后修正 "two borderline cases" 为准确数字。如果不确定精确数值，改用更安全的表述如 "borderline configurations may shift to K*=1"。

**问题 2：Table 14 "three families" vs 正文 "四组" 不一致**
正文 Section 3.2 说 15 个 features 分成四组（surprisal moments / surprisal derivatives / vocabulary diversity / frequency tail statistics）。但 Table 14 caption 说 "three families"。
修复方向：统一为一致的分类。建议改 Table 14 caption 为 "four groups"（与正文一致），或者如果 Table 14 确实只列了三大类（surprisal 含 moments+derivatives 作为一类），则正文也要相应调整。确保全文 feature taxonomy 一致。

编译确认后汇报。这次不用验证引用。

28	The theoretical upper bound $\Ktheory$ (the true maximum discriminable depth given optimal features) is not directly estimated in this work.
29	Since any specific classifier on a feature subset achieves AUC at most equal to the Bayes-optimal classifier on all features, $\Kempirical \leq \Ktheory$ follows directly.
30	The DPI motivates the empirical observation that $\auc_{k-1,k}$ generally decreases with $k$ under non-degenerate decoding, though AUC is not a monotone function of MI (as noted above).
31	Here $\auc_{k-1,k}$ is the point-estimate pairwise AUC and $\theta = 0.60$ is a reference operating point; $\theta$ sits between the 1v2 AUC cluster ($0.61$--$0.70$) and the 2v3 cluster ($0.55$--$0.59$), so that $\Kempirical$ is insensitive to the exact threshold within this range (justified below; Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).
32	All pairwise significance tests use 10{,}000-iteration permutation tests with Bonferroni correction for 10 comparisons ($\alpha_{\text{corrected}}{=}0.005$); we report bootstrap 95\% confidence intervals (CIs) (1{,}000 resamples unless otherwise noted) for all boundary-pair AUCs and explicitly mark marginal cases where Bonferroni-corrected lower bounds fall below $\theta$ (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
33	
34	\paragraph{Threshold justification.}
35	$\theta{=}0.60$ is a reference operating point supported by two independent lines of evidence.
36	First, a 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\,0.55$ ($p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons).
37	Second, $\Kempirical{=}2$ is insensitive to the exact threshold: 1v2 AUC values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, a ${\sim}$5--11\,pp discontinuity that ensures $\Kempirical$ is stable across $\theta \in [0.59, 0.60]$; at $\theta{=}0.61$, two borderline cases shift to $\Kempirical{=}1$.
38	Only at extremes does $\Kempirical$ shift broadly ($\theta{=}0.55$: $\Kempirical{=}3$ for two Qwen configurations; $\theta{=}0.70$: $\Kempirical{=}1$ across all).
39	Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta} provides the full sensitivity surface across $\theta \in [0.50, 0.70]$.
40	The downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}) uses the OBD classifier without re-optimizing $\theta$, avoiding circularity.
41	
42	\subsection{Features and Classifier}

we report bootstrap 95\\% confidence intervals (CIs) (1{,}000 resamples unless otherwise noted) for all boundary-pair AUCs and explicitly mark marginal cases where Bonferroni-corrected lower bounds fall below $\\theta$ (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n\n\\paragraph{Threshold justification.}\n$\\theta{=}0.60$ is a reference operating point supported by two independent lines of evidence.\nFirst, a 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\\,0.55$ ($p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons).\nSecond, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is insensitive to the exact threshold: 1v2 AUC values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, a ${\\sim}$5--11\\,pp discontinuity that ensures $\\Kempirical$ is stable across $\\theta \\in [0.59, 0.60]$; at $\\theta{=}0.61$, two borderline cases shift to $\\Kempirical{=}1$.\nOnly at extremes does $\\Kempirical$ shift broadly ($\\theta{=}0.55$: $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for two Qwen configurations; $\\theta{=}0.70$: $\\Kempirical{=}1$ across all).\nFigure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta} provides the full sensitivity surface across $\\theta \\in [0.50, 0.70]$.\nThe downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) uses the OBD classifier without re-optimizing $\\theta$, avoiding circularity.\n\n\\subsection{Features and Classifier}",
      "numLines": 15,
      "startLine": 28,
      "totalLines": 81
    }
  },

    "originalFile": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/pipeline.pdf}\n\\caption{Generational depth estimation pipeline. Seed documents are recursively passed through a generation operator $G_\\delta$ to produce a depth chain. Corpus-level statistical features are fed to the OBD-LightGBM classifier for depth prediction. (Simplified notation; see Eq.~\\ref{eq:kstar} for the formal $\\Kempirical$ definition.)}\n\\label{fig:pipeline}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\n\\label{sec:formulation}\n\nA generation operator $G_\\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\\delta$) produces successive generations $X \\to Y_1 \\to \\cdots \\to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \\triangleq X$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nThis sequence forms a Markov chain.\nBy the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \\geq I(X; Y_2) \\geq \\cdots \\geq 0$: mutual information with the seed text degrades monotonically.\nTogether with Markov chain convergence, this motivates the existence of a finite discrimination boundary where $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ falls below a decision threshold, though AUC and mutual information are not formally equivalent; the actual saturation depth must therefore be measured empirically.\nPairwise AUC generally decreases with depth but may exhibit noise-level reversals (e.g., Pythia-1.4B 3v4 $\\auc = 0.505$ vs.\\ 4v5 $= 0.508$, a difference within bootstrap CI; Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}), reinforcing that $\\Kempirical$ is a measured quantity rather than a theoretical prediction.\n\nWe define the operational lower bound:\n{\\small\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\Kempirical = \\max\\bigl\\{k \\!\\geq\\! 1 : \\auc_{j-1,j} > \\theta,\\;\\forall\\, 1 \\!\\leq\\! j \\!\\leq\\! k\\bigr\\},\n  \\label{eq:kstar}\n\\end{equation}\n}\nwhich requires all pairs up to depth $k$ to exceed the threshold (the $\\forall j$ constraint is naturally satisfied because $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ decreases monotonically above $\\theta$ across all tested configurations; Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci}).\nThe theoretical upper bound $\\Ktheory$ (the true maximum discriminable depth given optimal features) is not directly estimated in this work.\nSince any specific classifier on a feature subset achieves AUC at most equal to the Bayes-optimal classifier on all features, $\\Kempirical \\leq \\Ktheory$ follows directly.\nThe DPI motivates the empirical observation that $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ generally decreases with $k$ under non-degenerate decoding, though AUC is not a monotone function of MI (as noted above).\nHere $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ is the point-estimate pairwise AUC and $\\theta = 0.60$ is a reference operating point; $\\theta$ sits between the 1v2 AUC cluster ($0.61$--$0.70$) and the 2v3 cluster ($0.55$--$0.59$), so that $\\Kempirical$ is insensitive to the exact threshold within this range (justified below; Figure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).\nAll pairwise significance tests use 10{,}000-iteration permutation tests with Bonferroni correction for 10 comparisons ($\\alpha_{\\text{corrected}}{=}0.005$); we report bootstrap 95\\% confidence intervals (CIs) (1{,}000 resamples unless otherwise noted) for all boundary-pair AUCs and explicitly mark marginal cases where Bonferroni-corrected lower bounds fall below $\\theta$ (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n\n\\paragraph{Threshold justification.}\n$\\theta{=}0.60$ is a reference operating point supported by two independent lines of evidence.\nFirst, a 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\\,0.55$ ($p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons).\nSecond, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is insensitive to the exact threshold: 1v2 AUC values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, a ${\\sim}$5--11\\,pp discontinuity that ensures $\\Kempirical$ is stable across $\\theta \\in [0.59, 0.60]$; at $\\theta{=}0.61$, two borderline cases shift to $\\Kempirical{=}1$.\nOnly at extremes does $\\Kempirical$ shift broadly ($\\theta{=}0.55$: $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for two Qwen configurations; $\\theta{=}0.70$: $\\Kempirical{=}1$ across all).\nFigure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta} provides the full sensitivity surface across $\\theta \\in [0.50, 0.70]$.\nThe downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) uses the OBD classifier without re-optimizing $\\theta$, avoiding circularity.\n\n\\subsection{Features and Classifier}\n\\label{sec:features}\n\nEach document is characterized by a 15-dimensional feature vector spanning four groups: \\emph{surprisal moments} (mean, std, skewness, kurtosis; 4~dims), \\emph{surprisal derivatives} (mean and std of first- and second-order differences, plus first-order skewness; 5~dims), \\emph{vocabulary diversity} (type-token ratio (TTR), hapax legomena ratio, self-BLEU; 3~dims), and \\emph{frequency tail statistics} (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction; 3~dims).\nAll features are computed from token-level surprisal values via an external scorer model (full definitions in Table~\\ref{tab:s5_features}, Appendix).\nSelf-BLEU reference sets are drawn exclusively within each fold to prevent information leakage across cross-validation splits; self-BLEU ranks \\#1 in 1/8 conditions (greedy decoding) but is absent from the top-3 in all 7 others.\nRemoving self-BLEU reduces mean $\\auc_{1v2}$ by $< 1$\\,pp across all models (14D $\\Kempirical = 2$ for all 4 configurations); in deployment where reference corpora are unavailable, self-BLEU can be replaced with corpus-level self-similarity or omitted entirely, leaving 14 features (surprisal statistics, TTR, hapax ratio, frequency tail) that require no external reference and retain full $\\Kempirical$.\nThese features are generator-agnostic in that they require no knowledge of or access to the generating model, though they depend on an external scorer for surprisal computation (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\nWe use \\emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), decomposing the $(K{+}1)$-class problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers, each estimating $P(\\text{depth} > k \\mid \\mathbf{x})$ via LightGBM (hyperparameters in Table~\\ref{tab:s6_lgbm_params}).\nOBD achieves lower mean absolute error (MAE) than multiclass LightGBM (e.g., 0.984 vs.\\ 1.016 on Qwen-rewrite) while maintaining superior pairwise $\\auc$ (mean 0.6095 vs.\\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression) (Appendix~\\ref{sec:computation_cost}).\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:experimental_setup}\n\nWe evaluate six primary configurations spanning three model families (Table~\\ref{tab:setup}): Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation), Qwen2.5-1.5B (base), Qwen2.5-7B, Pythia-1.4B, and OLMo-1B; four additional models are introduced in Section~\\ref{sec:experiments}.\nEach uses 5{,}000 C4 seeds truncated to 256 tokens, recursively generated for $K{=}5$ iterations (30{,}000 documents per configuration; Table~\\ref{tab:s4_dataset_stats}).\nDefault decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; decoding ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.\nContinuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.\nEvaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\\auc$ as primary metric.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Experimental configurations for depth-chain generation. \\textbf{Config}: shorthand identifier; \\textbf{Model}: architecture and parameter count; \\textbf{Mode}: generation strategy (continuation or rewrite); $N$: number of seed documents per depth level.}\n\\label{tab:setup}\n\\begin{tabular}{llcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Config} & \\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-base & Qwen2.5-1.5B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nPythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nOLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-7B   & Qwen2.5-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n",

Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}), reinforcing that $\\Kempirical$ is a measured quantity rather than a theoretical prediction.\n\nWe define the operational lower bound:\n{\\small\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\Kempirical = \\max\\bigl\\{k \\!\\geq\\! 1 : \\auc_{j-1,j} > \\theta,\\;\\forall\\, 1 \\!\\leq\\! j \\!\\leq\\! k\\bigr\\},\n  \\label{eq:kstar}\n\\end{equation}\n}\nwhich requires all pairs up to depth $k$ to exceed the threshold (the $\\forall j$ constraint is naturally satisfied because $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ decreases monotonically above $\\theta$ across all tested configurations; Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci}).\nThe theoretical upper bound $\\Ktheory$ (the true maximum discriminable depth given optimal features) is not directly estimated in this work.\nSince any specific classifier on a feature subset achieves AUC at most equal to the Bayes-optimal classifier on all features, $\\Kempirical \\leq \\Ktheory$ follows directly.\nThe DPI motivates the empirical observation that $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ generally decreases with $k$ under non-degenerate decoding, though AUC is not a monotone function of MI (as noted above).\nHere $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ is the point-estimate pairwise AUC and $\\theta = 0.60$ is a reference operating point; $\\theta$ sits between the 1v2 AUC cluster ($0.607$--$0.696$) and the 2v3 cluster ($0.55$--$0.59$), so that $\\Kempirical$ is insensitive to the exact threshold within this range (justified below; Figure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta}).\nAll pairwise significance tests use 10{,}000-iteration permutation tests with Bonferroni correction for 10 comparisons ($\\alpha_{\\text{corrected}}{=}0.005$); we report bootstrap 95\\% confidence intervals (CIs) (1{,}000 resamples unless otherwise noted) for all boundary-pair AUCs and explicitly mark marginal cases where Bonferroni-corrected lower bounds fall below $\\theta$ (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n\n\\paragraph{Threshold justification.}\n$\\theta{=}0.60$ is a reference operating point supported by two independent lines of evidence.\nFirst, a 10{,}000-iteration permutation test yields a 95th-percentile null of ${<}\\,0.55$ ($p < 0.002$ for all boundary comparisons).\nSecond, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is insensitive to the exact threshold: 1v2 AUC values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, a ${\\sim}$5--11\\,pp discontinuity that ensures $\\Kempirical$ is stable across $\\theta \\in [0.59, 0.60]$; at $\\theta{=}0.61$, two borderline cases shift to $\\Kempirical{=}1$.\nOnly at extremes does $\\Kempirical$ shift broadly ($\\theta{=}0.55$: $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for two Qwen configurations; $\\theta{=}0.70$: $\\Kempirical{=}1$ across all).\nFigure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta} provides the full sensitivity surface across $\\theta \\in [0.50, 0.70]$.\nThe downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) uses the OBD classifier without re-optimizing $\\theta$, avoiding circularity.\n\n\\subsection{Features and Classifier}\n\\label{sec:features}\n\nEach document is characterized by a 15-dimensional feature vector spanning four groups: \\emph{surprisal moments} (mean, std, skewness, kurtosis; 4~dims), \\emph{surprisal derivatives} (mean and std of first- and second-order differences, plus first-order skewness; 5~dims), \\emph{vocabulary diversity} (type-token ratio (TTR), hapax legomena ratio, self-BLEU; 3~dims), and \\emph{frequency tail statistics} (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction; 3~dims).\nAll features are computed from token-level surprisal values via an external scorer model (full definitions in Table~\\ref{tab:s5_features}, Appendix).\nSelf-BLEU reference sets are drawn exclusively within each fold to prevent information leakage across cross-validation splits; self-BLEU ranks \\#1 in 1/8 conditions (greedy decoding) but is absent from the top-3 in all 7 others.\nRemoving self-BLEU reduces mean $\\auc_{1v2}$ by $< 1$\\,pp across all models (14D $\\Kempirical = 2$ for all 4 configurations); in deployment where reference corpora are unavailable, self-BLEU can be replaced with corpus-level self-similarity or omitted entirely, leaving 14 features (surprisal statistics, TTR, hapax ratio, frequency tail) that require no external reference and retain full $\\Kempirical$.\nThese features are generator-agnostic in that they require no knowledge of or access to the generating model, though they depend on an external scorer for surprisal computation (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\nWe use \\emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), decomposing the $(K{+}1)$-class problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers, each estimating $P(\\text{depth} > k \\mid \\mathbf{x})$ via LightGBM (hyperparameters in Table~\\ref{tab:s6_lgbm_params}).\nOBD achieves lower mean absolute error (MAE) than multiclass LightGBM (e.g., 0.984 vs.\\ 1.016 on Qwen-rewrite) while maintaining superior pairwise $\\auc$ (mean 0.6095 vs.\\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression) (Appendix~\\ref{sec:computation_cost}).\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:experimental_setup}\n\nWe evaluate six primary configurations spanning three model families (Table~\\ref{tab:setup}): Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct (rewrite and continuation), Qwen2.5-1.5B (base), Qwen2.5-7B, Pythia-1.4B, and OLMo-1B; four additional models are introduced in Section~\\ref{sec:experiments}.\nEach uses 5{,}000 C4 seeds truncated to 256 tokens, recursively generated for $K{=}5$ iterations (30{,}000 documents per configuration; Table~\\ref{tab:s4_dataset_stats}).\nDefault decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; decoding ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.\nContinuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.\nEvaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\\auc$ as primary metric.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Experimental configurations for depth-chain generation. \\textbf{Config}: shorthand identifier; \\textbf{Model}: architecture and parameter count; \\textbf{Mode}: generation strategy (continuation or rewrite); $N$: number of seed documents per depth level.}\n\\label{tab:setup}\n\\begin{tabular}{llcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Config} & \\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-base & Qwen2.5-1.5B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nPythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nOLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-7B   & Qwen2.5-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n",
